## Current Batch Checkpoint (2026-10-09)

`default` ID 65134. Coverage is 73,430/73,430 across 1,428 preliminary
reports: 57 `base`, 60 contiguous `affix` batches, one `affix` fallback
follow-up, and 1,310 `default` batches. Both the contiguous `default` scan
and focused comparison of all 140 Chinese-only `affix` fallbacks are
complete as preliminary work; independent review/disposition remains open.

Older batch-level `Resume at ID`, `next ID`, and cursor statements in this
running log describe their historical checkpoints, not the current work
state. The current scan is complete through `default` ID 65134; no
preliminary corpus rows or variants remain unscanned.

### Expanded Combined-Name Follow-Up (2026-10-09)

The user clarified that `base` and `affix` combine into a single displayed
item name, so their Korean wording, tone, order, and any English-parenthetical
policy must be checked together. Round 1,
[`combined-name-review-00000-00099-round1.md`](combined-name-review-00000-00099-round1.md),
covers `base` IDs 0-99 and all 60 target variants for `affix` IDs 0-9.
Japanese source strings are emphasized for affix slot/variant structure;
English remains the semantic authority and Chinese supporting evidence.
Findings are preliminary and no translation CSV was edited.

Round 2,
[`combined-name-review-00100-00199-round2.md`](combined-name-review-00100-00199-round2.md),
covers `base` IDs 100-199 and all 60 target variants for `affix` IDs 10-19.
It flags `Crushing` and `of Sundering` both translated as `분쇄의`, despite
distinct English/Japanese source forms. Cumulative follow-up coverage is
200/4,844 base rows and 120/3,451 affix variants.

[`combined-name-review-00200-00299-round3.md`](combined-name-review-00200-00299-round3.md),

English/Japanese disagreement at `Lambent`. Cumulative follow-up coverage is

Cumulative follow-up coverage is 600/4,844 base rows and 345/3,451 affix
variants (945/8,295 combined).

Resume at `base` IDs 1100-1199 and `affix` IDs 110-119, then proceed
sequentially through all 4,844 base rows and 3,451 affix variants. Only after
that, carry the user-requested natural literary, figurative, satirical, and
idiomatic localization criterion through the integrated `default` review.

The combined-name pass currently uses locale source forms and likely affix
slots as linguistic evidence. The inspected loot/item-set assets are opaque
binaries; an item-specific base/affix pairing table and runtime-rendered
combined names have not been verified. Treat attachment examples as
hypotheses, not observed game output.

## Scope and Evidence

- Active files: `work/zh_cn/locatexts_zh_cn.koalocatext_{base,affix,default}.csv`.
- English (`src/csv/en_us/`) is the primary source for meaning.
- Japanese (`src/csv/ja_jp/`) is the primary reference for voice, register,
  honorifics, and character tone.
- Chinese (`src/csv/zh_cn/`) is a supporting meaning check, not an authority
  over English or Japanese.
- The official Korean manual and established project terms take precedence
  for Korean names and terminology.
- This preliminary work is read-only. Do not edit working CSVs until individual findings
  have an approved disposition.
- This is not the independent Reviewer pass defined in `TRANSLATION-ROLE.md`.
  Findings here are preliminary candidates and do not authorize an Editor pass.

## Historical Audit Reconciliation

The prior Japanese-derived audit is part of this review's evidence and must
not be discarded:

- `work/ja_jp/audit/open-questions.md` and
  `work/zh_cn/audit/from-ja_jp/open-questions.md` are byte-identical.
- The historical open-question log has 578 source-key-checked `default`
  ranges covering 59,935 of 65,135 IDs. This is an issue inventory, not a
  linguistic approval of every row in those ranges.
- The unrecorded historical ranges are IDs 0-799, 16,300-16,399,
  17,500-17,599, 25,000-25,099, 26,050-26,099, 27,000-27,099,
  32,700-33,399, 37,700-40,349, 42,850-42,949, and 45,050-45,549.
- The two historical review reports for IDs 0-49 and 50-99 are also
  byte-identical to their archived copies under `from-ja_jp/`. Their key
  findings were checked against the active `zh_cn` rows below. The reports'
  “no change needed” decisions remain historical evidence and do not replace
  the consolidated review of the active corpus.

### Historical `default` IDs 0-99

| IDs | Historical disposition | Active `zh_cn` status |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 2, 17, 20, 25, 40, 41 | Accepted findings in the IDs 0-49 report | Current Korean matches the recorded applied wording. |
| 1, 29, 32, 45, 46 | Uncertain in the IDs 0-49 report | Still unresolved; retain for the new review. |
| 56, 83 | Accepted findings in the IDs 50-99 report | The proposed corrections are **not present** in the active CSV. Reassess and disposition them in this review; do not assume the old “accepted” label means the migrated file was corrected. |
| 65, 89, 95 | Uncertain in the IDs 50-99 report | Still unresolved; retain for the new review. |

The historical report proposes the following Korean for the two accepted
findings that were not propagated to the active file:

| ID | Current active Korean | Historical suggested Korean | Issue |
| ---: | --- | --- | --- |
| 56 | 너 죽은 거야, 알아들어? 끝장이라고! 배를 갈라 내장을 끄집어내고, 네 내장으로 목을 졸라 죽여 주마! | 너 죽은 거야, 알아들어? 끝장이라고! 배를 갈라 내장을 끄집어내 네 목구멍에 처넣어 질식시켜 주마! | English says the victim is choked with their own entrails; the current Korean changes this to strangling with them. |
| 83 | 이걸 가져오느라 고생했겠지만, 결국 파기되는 모습을 지켜봐야 하는군. 그래도 반드시 해야 해. 이해하겠지? | 내게 이걸 가져오느라 고생했는데, 결국 파기되는 걸 지켜봐야 하는군. 그래도 반드시 해야 해. 이해하겠지? | English describes effort already made; the current Korean makes it hypothetical. |

These remain preliminary high-confidence candidates, not applied edits. The
0-99 range was reread once as an initial sample; that pass does not replace a
later independent, consolidated Reviewer assessment.

### Historical Translation-Map Conflicts

The historical audit identified three `default` IDs where
`work/zh_cn/.default-translations.tsv` differs from the active CSV. The values
were rechecked directly; none was silently synchronized:

| ID | Active `zh_cn` | Preserved map | Current assessment |
| ---: | --- | --- | --- |
| 51421 | `계승자(Scion)` | `후계자(Scion)` | Both are possible Korean renderings, but the in-world office `Scion` is inconsistent throughout the corpus. Decide it through the terminology review, not by copying the map. |
| 51603 | `교수대 끝(Gallows End)` | `갤로우스 엔드(Gallows End)` | The English is a place name. Compare this with the many other renderings of `Gallows End`; the active literal form is a strong consistency/naturalness concern. |
| 63500 | The final lines say Belmaid cloaks herself, then looks at the castle. | The final lines say she sees the castle, then cloaks herself. | The English verse and Japanese both place seeing the castle before her concealment. The active Korean reverses that order; the map version is a high-confidence candidate correction. |

These conflicts remain pending disposition. In particular, do not sync the
map wholesale: one text may be a deliberate manual edit, and the map itself
is not an independent authority.

## Proper-Name Bilingual Marking

The project instructions do not define when a proper name must appear as
`한글(English)`. The historical review used parentheses selectively when a
rendering was uncertain or English traceability was useful; this explains
some variation but does not provide a consistent user-facing rule.

A read-only pattern scan of the active Korean text found:

| Resource | Rows matching Hangul followed by an English parenthetical | Matching occurrences |
| --- | ---: | ---: |
| `base` | 1,331 | 1,342 |
| `affix` | 0 | 0 |
| `default` | 12,815 | 17,784 |

This is only a format-frequency baseline. It neither identifies every proper
name nor determines whether an occurrence should be bilingual. During review,
record the entity, Korean form, English spelling, resource/ID, and whether the
same entity is marked consistently. Do not add or remove parenthetical names
globally until a project-wide display convention is approved.

### High-Impact Name/Term Variants Found

A source-keyed scan of `default` found 196 rows containing `Gallows End`.
Across those rows, Korean forms include `갤로스 엔드`, `갤로우스 엔드`,
`갤로우즈 엔드`, `갈로스 엔드`, `갤러스 엔드`, `갤로즈 엔드`, and literal
forms such as `교수대 끝`, `교수대의 끝`, and `교수대 끝자락`. This is a
confirmed consistency problem; the canonical Korean form still needs a
terminology decision before any normalization.

The source term `Scion` occurs in 146 `default` rows. In-world title usages
vary among `사이온`, `후계자`, `계승자`, `사이언`, and `스키온`; some rows
also use `자손`. A few source uses may mean a generic descendant rather than
the Cape Solace religious office, so separate senses before deciding one
Korean term. These counts are a source-keyed inventory, not a recommendation
to replace every occurrence with one form.

## Preliminary Coverage

Historical notes, structural checks, and current preliminary work are tracked
separately. Preliminary coverage means a row has received one first-pass
reading against English meaning, Japanese voice/register, Chinese supporting
evidence, and applicable Korean terminology. It does not mean the row is
approved or formally reviewed.

| Resource | Rows / variants | Preliminary coverage |
| --- | ---: | --- |
| `base` | 4,844 | IDs 0-4,843 read once (4,844 rows); findings remain open. |
| `affix` | 3,451 | IDs 0-585 read once; all 140 Chinese-only fallback variants were also cross-checked (35 in prior reports, 105 in the supplemental report); findings remain open. |
| `default` | 65,135 | IDs 0-65,134 read once (65,135 rows); findings remain open. Historical notes for other IDs are inputs, not approvals. |

The current reconciled total is 73,430/73,430 rows or variants across 1,428
preliminary reports (57 `base`, 60 contiguous `affix` batches, one
supplemental fallback report, and 1,310 `default` batches), matching direct
audit-directory counts. Earlier progress subtotals were inconsistent; the
directory count is authoritative. No preliminary row/variant remains
unscanned. All findings remain preliminary/open.

terms, Headsman duel, English source typo, Sandstone Villa note, shipboard
journal, and Executioner question. This remains preparatory coverage, not
independent linguistic review.
The 24551-24600 batch records the Quay/Motus Mining distinction, House and
Maid terms, Witchknight/Slumber, the Gravehal evacuation letter, the Counsel
and Testament questions, and the seven-day diary. This remains preparatory
coverage, not independent linguistic review.
The 24501-24550 batch records Will-o'-the-Wisp and place-name consistency,
the archive-key wordplay, the silkfarmer diary, Quake values, wartime/lore
wording, and faction/name questions. This remains preparatory coverage, not
independent linguistic review.
The 24451-24500 batch records the First's crypt, Testament/Pledgeshields
source conflicts, Wrath values, material categories, Hyperian names, Delvers
wordplay, Court of Enchantments, and Traveler/Moon Camp questions. This
remains preparatory coverage, not independent linguistic review.
The 24401-24450 batch includes the Fae Memorial, stone-count ambiguity,
Fateweaver robe, encounter/debug label, block-stat term, Cairn, attrition
strategy, and dark-joke questions.

### Coverage Reconciliation

The prior running total undercounted `affix` IDs 0-569 by 110 variants and
therefore understated preliminary coverage. The range-by-range reconciliation
checked the 323 linked batch reports through IDs 9949 (57 `base`, 60
`affix`, 206 `default`):
each contains a read-only/completed-pass statement, its documented ID range
maps to active `zh_cn` keys, and the ranges have no gaps or overlaps within
their documented scope. The `default` reports now form continuous coverage
from ID 0 through 9949. The additional report for IDs 9950-9999 was checked
against active English, Japanese, Chinese, and Korean keys, bringing the
linked report total to 334 (217 `default` reports); coverage is now continuous
through ID 10499. This verifies documented preliminary-pass coverage and
structural scope only; it is not an independent linguistic review.

The ten reports for `default` IDs 10500-10999 extend the contiguous,
report-backed reading through ID 10999. The report for IDs 11000-11049 was
then checked against all four locale files and the historical question log:
all 50 keys occur in identical order, and line-break counts plus normalized
runtime-marker class/order signatures match. Its new candidates include
`Kollossae`/`Eventide`/`Truesworn` spelling consistency, the Sorrows/House
referents, and a likely `노움`/`놈` typo at ID 11045. These remain preliminary
questions, not approved corrections.

The CSV-level line-break and runtime-marker checks were also re-run for
`default` IDs 6100-6149, 6300-6399, 6600-6649, 6800-6849, 7000-7049, and
7100-8749. Actual multiline CSV records were parsed. Line-break counts
matched across English, Japanese, Chinese, and Korean except at IDs 7257
and 8670:
Japanese has two embedded line breaks, while English, Chinese, and Korean
have three at ID 7257. At ID 8670, Chinese has three breaks while English,
Japanese, and Korean have four. The Korean poem at 7257 follows English and
Chinese. Localized marker ID/order sequences, other brace tokens, and named
icon markers matched except at ID 8587: Japanese omits `{LINE_CENTER}` and
places its first localized
`{*1...}` marker before `{1}`, while Korean matches English and Chinese.
The exception is recorded in
[`default-ids-08550-08599-preliminary.md`](default-ids-08550-08599-preliminary.md).
The official Korean manual PDF could not be text-searched in this environment,
so established-term confirmation for IDs 8350-10499 remains pending. These
checks do not establish linguistic correctness.

The `affix` reports for IDs 340-349 and 360-369 previously claimed 60 rows
each. The active CSV contains 55 rows in each range because ID 342 and ID 361,
respectively, have variant 11 only. Those reports were corrected to the
actual key counts; no active CSV was changed. The eight later batches
(`affix` 570-579 and 580-585; `default` 2600-2649, 2650-2699, 2700-2749,
2750-2799, 2800-2849, and 2850-2899) were also checked against their active
keys and linked in the audit index.

Preliminary questions include the `awareness` UI label (10502), the
Testament as an apparent organization rather than `유언서` (10528), the
unsupported younger-brother reading for Gilman (10531), the rite named

`poachers` readings in Japanese and Chinese (10549). These remain
unconfirmed candidates. The next batch also flags the likely `Cape Soalce`
source typo and Gallows End spelling variation (10552), the unit for three
`parcels` (10555), conflicting rite readings (10572), and the unresolved
one-word `Even` label (10597). The latest batches add terminology questions
for the Maid, Widow, Revival, and recipe materials (10601, 10614, 10625,
10627), the meaning of `a millennium of sorrows` and `Delve` (10634, 10658),
the one-word `Preordained` label (10679), and a Mithros spelling variation
(10698).
The latest batch also records ambiguity in the three-person traitor reference
(10702), the `Queen of Cups` label (10729), and the historical chronology and
name spellings in ID 10739. ID 10790's encoded text is identical in English,
Japanese, and Korean but differs in the Chinese source, which has one fewer
line break. The latest batch also flags the ambiguous `The Font` label
(10833), name variants for Gwalchmai (10831), and the long city-history and
Tyr Magnus passages (10838, 10842). The newest report also flags the
Circlet of Arkes item type (10863), the unexplained Scale mechanism (10870),
and the ironic house-use statement (10876).
